| `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R009.101.1` | **Index a batch of harness sessions.** Class: BUILD. Types, in new `crates/medulla-types/src/harness_session.rs` (derive `Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize`, `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]`, `schemars::JsonSchema` under `contract-schema`): `HarnessSessionRecord { harness_id: String, adapter_revision: u32, source_session_id: String, run_trace_id: String, run_trace_revision: u64, trace_digest: String, source_sequence: u64 }`; `UnavailableSession { harness_id: String, source_session_id: String, reason: String }`; `IndexHarnessSessionBatch { tenant_id: String, manifest_id: String, manifest_revision: u32, manifest_digest: String, declared_total: u64, sync_receipt_id: String, sync_receipt_digest: String, source_cursor: String, records: Vec<HarnessSessionRecord>, unavailable: Vec<UnavailableSession> }`; `IndexHarnessSessionBatchResult { indexed: u32, unchanged: u32, unavailable: u32, index_revision: u64 }`. Bounds: ids at most 256 chars; `source_cursor` at most 256; `reason` at most 512; `records.len() + unavailable.len()` at most the knob `orchestration_limits.session_batch_max` (default 500, range [1, 5000]), larger refuses `PARAMETER_INVALID`; digests are 64 lowercase hex characters without prefix. Wire: `Method::IndexHarnessSessionBatch { batch: IndexHarnessSessionBatch }` in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_11.rs`, result contract `IndexHarnessSessionBatchResult`, write policy as a graph write, generated Python models and client method `index_harness_session_batch`. Admission `pub fn admit_batch` and `pub fn run_trace_digest` in new `crates/medulla-h-ingest-repository/src/harness_session.rs` (pure over the graph core, so the crate below the router owns the rules), committed by the gateway route `Route::IndexHarnessSessionBatch` of `crates/medulla-h-graph-gateway/src/graph_ops/harness_session.rs` beside `ProcessDocumentImport` (DECIDED ORCH w7 r5: the route is where a graph write gets its mutation context and compare-and-set, so there is no second commit path). `tenant_id` must equal the verified tenant (any other value reads as absent: `SESSION_TRACE_UNKNOWN`). Verification of every record, in this order, writing nothing on the first refusal: (1) `SESSION_MANIFEST_UNSUPPORTED` when `manifest_revision` is not 1 (the constant `HARNESS_SESSION_MANIFEST_REVISION`), a `manifest_digest`, `sync_receipt_digest` or `trace_digest` is not 64 lowercase hex, or a record's `adapter_revision` is 0; (2) `SESSION_TRACE_UNKNOWN` when `run_trace_id` is not the id of a node the caller can see whose `node_type` is `RunTrace` in the caller's tenant (a node of another tenant reads as absent); (3) `SESSION_CONTENT_CHANGED` when the RunTrace's stored `run_revision` property (a decimal integer, read as `u64`; a trace that stores none, such as one written by the terminal work-item commit `apply_receipt_rows` in `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/commit.rs`, counts as revision 1) differs from `run_trace_revision`, or when `trace_digest` differs from the digest the handler computes from the stored trace node. There is no `RunTraceStep` type and no `step_index` property on main, and steps are stored two ways (inline in the `run_trace` property as `TraceStep { index, kind, action_ref, outcome, arguments }`, or as `hasStep` nodes of a generic write), so the digest does not read steps. It is `pub fn run_trace_digest(properties: &serde_json::Map<String, serde_json::Value>) -> String` in `crates/medulla-h-ingest-repository/src/harness_session.rs`: the lowercase hex SHA-256 of the canonical JSON (object keys sorted at every depth, no whitespace, `serde_json` numbers) of the RunTrace record's property map with the keys `node_type`, `tenant` and `run_revision` removed. The digest is defined over the canonical submitted record, the property map the connector wrote when it created the RunTrace node, and never over properties read back from the engine (the engine may add or hide keys of its own); a connector computes the same function over the record it submitted, and the L4 test computes `trace_digest` from the record it submitted. The contract `contracts/scion-harness-session-ingest.md` is updated to this definition in the same commit. The index writes, as one `BatchUpdate` through the ordinary commit path: the receipt node `harnesssync:<tenant>:<sync_receipt_id>` (properties `manifest_id`, `manifest_revision`, `manifest_digest`, `declared_total`, `sync_receipt_digest`, `source_cursor`), one session node `harnesssession:<tenant>:<harness_id>:<source_session_id>` per record with `type` `HarnessSession`, `status` `indexed` and the record fields, one session node per `UnavailableSession` with `status` `unavailable`, `reason` and no trace, one edge `indexesRun` from each indexed session node to its RunTrace node, and the per-tenant counter node `harnesssessionindex:<tenant>` whose `revision` is raised by one when the batch changed anything. Every integer property on these index nodes is a decimal string (index nodes are string maps). Idempotency and change rules: replaying a batch whose receipt id, receipt digest and every record are identical changes nothing (`unchanged` counts the records, `index_revision` is unchanged); the same `sync_receipt_id` with a different `sync_receipt_digest` refuses `SESSION_CONTENT_CHANGED`; for an existing session node, an equal `source_sequence` with any other field differing, or a lower `source_sequence`, refuses `SESSION_CONTENT_CHANGED`, and a higher `source_sequence` replaces the node. A session node that is `unavailable` and later arrives as a record is replaced by the `indexed` node. A replay after restart behaves the same because all of this state is in the graph. Codes `SESSION_MANIFEST_UNSUPPORTED`, `SESSION_CONTENT_CHANGED`, `SESSION_TRACE_UNKNOWN` and `SESSION_CURSOR_STALE` are the table `medulla_types::harness_session::REFUSALS` (collected with every family table, as the run-trace export codes are) and are routed for `IndexHarnessSessionBatch` in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`; the knob `orchestration_limits.page_size_max` is the existing registry entry and `orchestration_limits.session_batch_max` (default 500, range [1, 5000]) is added beside it in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/knob_config.rs`. An unavailable entry never replaces an indexed session and an equal unavailable entry is unchanged; the receipt node also carries the `index_revision` it was written at. Does not reuse `crates/medulla-h-ingest-repository/src/source_batch.rs` (the SQL path has no sync receipt). Writes: index nodes, receipt node, counter node and `indexesRun` edges. Depends: SCION-SOURCE-INGEST-R013.5. Caller chain: `connector-sync --once` (SCION-SOURCE-INGEST-R013.3) → `SourceIngestChannel.submit` → engine client `index_harness_session_batch` → `Method::IndexHarnessSessionBatch` → `index_batch`. Shape: the types, the method variant and `index_batch` as above; wire vectors for the types are committed beside them. | Test: `crates/medulla-types/src/harness_session.rs::tests::batch_round_trips_against_vectors` (L2). Test: `tests/orchestration/test_harness_session_index_served.py::test_replay_is_idempotent_and_changed_content_refuses` (L4): commits a `RunTrace` with two steps through `add_node`/`add_edge`, indexes a batch, replays it (unchanged), re-sends one record with the same `source_sequence` and another `trace_digest` (`SESSION_CONTENT_CHANGED`), sends an unknown trace (`SESSION_TRACE_UNKNOWN`) and `manifest_revision` 2 (`SESSION_MANIFEST_UNSUPPORTED`), and asserts a second tenant's identical `run_trace_id` reads as absent. |

| `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R009.101.2` | **Search harness sessions as a UQL view over the full manifest.** Class: BUILD. Caller chain: arbora `sessions.search` (ARBORA-OPS-R060) → engine client `uql` → `Method::Uql` → the statement `harness_session_search` in the `HARNESS_QUERIES` table of `crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/harness_metrics.rs`. Shape: one statement over the index nodes of R009.101.1 (`harnesssession:*`, `harnesssync:*`, `harnesssessionindex:*`); it reuses the snapshot cursor of R010.101 and names no handler. The linked RunTrace's `endMs` is the node property declared by `RunTraceBudgetShape` of `orchestration-v1.shapes.ttl` (`:endMs`, integer); no step property is read. Params: `text` (optional, at most 256 chars; case-insensitive substring match against `source_session_id` and `harness_id`, the only text an index node holds), `harness_id` (optional exact match), `from_ms` and `to_ms` (optional half-open window over the linked RunTrace's `endMs`; a session whose trace has no `endMs` is excluded when either bound is set), `page_size` (at most `orchestration_limits.page_size_max`) and `cursor`. Result: the first page (no cursor) begins with one `summary` row, then one `entry` row per session ordered by `(source_sequence desc, source_session_id asc)`. Columns: `kind` (`summary` or `entry`), then for the summary `index_revision` (the `revision` of the tenant's `harnesssessionindex` node, 0 when none), `declared`, `received`, `indexed`, `unavailable`, `pending`, `watermark` (the `source_cursor` of the newest receipt by `index_revision`) and `cost_status`; for an entry `harness_id`, `source_session_id`, `status` (`indexed` or `unavailable`), `reason` (empty unless unavailable), `run_trace_id`, `end_ms` and `source_sequence`. Counts over the filtered manifest: `declared` is the sum of `declared_total` of the newest receipt of each `manifest_id`; `received` is the number of session nodes (indexed plus unavailable); `indexed` and `unavailable` count by `status`; `pending = declared - received` (never negative); a declared session that has not arrived stays in the denominator as `pending`, and an unavailable source stays in `declared` and `received`. Cost totals: the summary row carries `cost_status` `measured` only when every indexed session's RunTrace carries an `accounting_revision` and a price in the pinned `PriceTable` for each of its models, and then the totals of R010.101 (`uncached_input`, `cached_input`, `output`, `reasoning_output`, `cost_minor`, `currency`, `accounting_revision`) computed by the same aggregation function `run_trace_usage` uses, restricted to the matching traces; otherwise `cost_status` is `unknown` with `cost_reason` `price_missing` or `attribution_missing` and the totals columns are null, never 0. Cursor: the snapshot cursor binds tenant, the filters and `index_revision`; a cursor whose bound `index_revision` differs from the current one refuses `SESSION_CURSOR_STALE`; a cursor of another tenant or filter refuses `SESSION_CURSOR_STALE` too. A foreign tenant's sessions, receipts and traces are invisible, so they affect no count and no row. Serving: the pure function `harness_session_search` of `crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/harness_session_search.rs` (statement `harness_session_search` of `HARNESS_QUERIES`), served by `handle_harness_session_search` in `crates/medulla-h-query-read/src/query/harness_metrics.rs`. Every column of a row travels as provenance text, the row id being the session node id (`summary` for the first row), as the capability gap rows do, and the cursor of the next page is the warning `next_cursor: <cursor>`. A cost total is measured only when every indexed session's trace carries the same `accounting_revision` and a `PriceTable` of that revision is visible; the aggregation is `run_trace_usage` over the matching traces, and a column it cannot price or count is `unknown` (`price_missing` or `attribution_missing`). `declared` is not narrowed by a filter. Writes: none. Depends: R009.101.1, R010.101. | Test: `tests/orchestration/test_harness_session_index_served.py::test_search_covers_manifest_with_unavailable_third_source` (L4): a manifest declaring 3 sessions, two indexed and one `unavailable`, returns `declared` 3, `received` 3, `indexed` 2, `unavailable` 1, `pending` 0; a second batch declaring 4 leaves `pending` 1. Test: `tests/orchestration/test_harness_session_index_served.py::test_cost_totals_report_unknown_not_zero` (L4): a trace without price yields `cost_status` `unknown` with null totals; a cursor minted before a new batch refuses `SESSION_CURSOR_STALE`. |
Depends: MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R009.101.1, MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R010.101

| `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R012.7.1` | **Model: policy rule and candidate with source spans, and the document grammar.** Class: BUILD. Caller chain: scoped `IndexRepository` → `route_repository_index` → `project_spec_artifacts` (`MEDULLA-SDLC-R014.2`) → `classify_spec_files` (R012.4), whose `SpecPolicy` nodes are the policy branch → `parse_policy_document`. Location: `crates/medulla-shacl/src/policy.rs` is the ICV write guard and stays so; the parser does not go there. New file `crates/medulla-agent-types/src/policy_document.rs` (declared `pub mod policy_document;` in that crate's `lib.rs`; the crate already owns `GuardrailRule` and depends on `medulla-types` and `sha2`). It holds the records and the parser. Records (each `Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize`, `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]`, `contract-schema` derive like `GuardrailRule`): `PolicySeverity { Info, Warn, Block }` (serde `snake_case`); `PolicyRule { rule_id: String, scope: String, severity: PolicySeverity, source_ref: PolicySourceRef, clause_digest: String }`; `PolicySourceRef { repository: String, path: String, commit: String, blob_digest: String, start_line: u32, end_line: u32 }` (lines 1-based, inclusive); `PolicyCandidateStatus { Proposed, Inactive { reason: PolicyInactiveReason } }` and `PolicyInactiveReason { ClauseUnparsed, ClauseAmbiguous }` (serde `SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE`: `CLAUSE_UNPARSED`, `CLAUSE_AMBIGUOUS`); `PolicyCandidate { rule: Option<PolicyRule>, source_ref: PolicySourceRef, candidate_set_digest: String, grammar_revision: u32, status: PolicyCandidateStatus }` (`rule` is `None` exactly when the status is `Inactive`). `pub const POLICY_GRAMMAR_REVISION: u32 = 1;`. Signature: `pub fn parse_policy_document(bytes: &[u8], profile: PolicyProfile, source: &PolicySourceRef) -> Result<Vec<PolicyCandidate>, PolicyDocumentError>` with `pub enum PolicyProfile { Constitution, RuleSet }` and `PolicyDocumentError::NotUtf8` (a non-UTF-8 document is refused as a whole; every other fault is a per-clause inactive candidate). `source` carries repository, path, commit and blob digest; the parser fills each candidate's `start_line` and `end_line`. Grammar at `grammar_revision` 1 (the one this row defines; the stored `GuardrailRule` of R127.2.1 is what a `Proposed` candidate becomes at synthesis, with `applies_to_class` = `scope` and `source_generation` = `grammar-1`). Line endings are normalized to LF first. `Constitution` profile: a clause is one Markdown list item (a line starting with `- `, `* ` or `<digits>. `, plus its indented continuation lines, which join with single spaces) whose text begins with a normative keyword, in `ABNF`: `clause = keyword SP text [SP scope]`, `keyword = "MUST NOT" / "MUST" / "SHOULD NOT" / "SHOULD" / "MAY"` (upper case, whole words), `scope = "{scope=" 1*(%x21-7A / %x7C / %x7E) "}"` (an absolute IRI: one scheme, a colon, no whitespace). Severity: `MUST NOT` and `MUST` give `block`; `SHOULD NOT` and `SHOULD` give `warn`; `MAY` gives `info`. List items that do not begin with a keyword are not clauses and produce no candidate (prose never becomes a rule). `RuleSet` profile (the bundled mini-markdown): one rule per line `rule <id> <info|warn|block> <scope>: <text>`, where `<id>` matches `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{1,63}$` and `<scope>` is an absolute IRI; blank lines and lines starting with `#` are skipped; any other non-blank line is a candidate with status `Inactive { ClauseUnparsed }`. Per-clause faults (status `Inactive`, `rule` None): a keyword clause with no `{scope=...}` or with a scope that is not an absolute IRI is `CLAUSE_UNPARSED`; a keyword clause with more than one `{scope=...}`, or whose text holds both a strong word (`MUST`, `MUST NOT`) and a weak word (`SHOULD`, `SHOULD NOT`, `MAY`) as whole upper-case words after the leading keyword, is `CLAUSE_AMBIGUOUS`. Identity: `clause_digest` is lowercase hex SHA-256 of the clause text after trimming and collapsing whitespace runs to one space (without the scope annotation); `rule_id` of a `Constitution` clause is `policy-` plus the first 16 hex digits of `clause_digest`, of a `RuleSet` line its `<id>`; `candidate_set_digest` (the same value on every candidate of one document) is lowercase hex SHA-256 over the candidates' `clause_digest` strings (inactive candidates included, using the digest of their clause text), sorted ascending and joined by `\n`. A `Constitution` document and a `RuleSet` document that state the same clause and scope give equal `PolicyRule` values apart from `source_ref`. Time is O(bytes). Shape: the file above and the `lib.rs` line. Depends: none. | Test: `crates/medulla-agent-types/src/policy_document.rs::tests::candidate_round_trips_and_inactive_has_reason` (L1) and `crates/medulla-agent-types/src/policy_document.rs::tests::both_formats_parse_to_one_type` (L1; the two profiles parse `MUST NOT commit secrets {scope=https://example.invalid/class/Repo}` and `rule no-secrets block https://example.invalid/class/Repo: commit secrets` to rules with equal `severity`, `scope` and `rule_id` differing only by identity rule, and spans match the lines; ambiguous and unparsed clauses carry their reason). |
Depends: MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R012.4, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R127.2.1
| `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R012.7.2` | **Wiring: synthesize on repository index.** Class: BUILD. Proposal path: the engine-side `ProposeRule` op of `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R129.100` is built in `medulla-h-decide`, which depends on `medulla-h-ingest`, so synthesis cannot call it. The `proposed` candidates are therefore written as nodes here, and the `ProposeRule` call is made by a reviewer or by arbora from those nodes (`DecisionLog` `ProposeRule` carries the node's `rule_id`, `scope`, `source_output_digest` = the candidate's `clause_digest`). | `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R012.7.3` | **Activation: invariant guard, review and atomic publish.** Stage: 3 Policy and constraints then 9 Commit, ops `Decide` kind `Promotion` and `DecisionLog` `learn`. Before the move, `check_rule_against_invariants` (R129.3) verifies every rule against the pinned invariant generation. The activated set is one `GovernedSet` revision: the champion pointer `champion:governed_set:policy:<repository>` names `policyset:<candidate_set_digest>` and one compare-and-set row moves it, so the move is atomic and each refusal keeps the prior set. No per-rule `ApproveRule` is written: that op needs two verified principals, while the review record carries its author and reviewer and `subject_gate` requires them to differ, which is the same two-person control (DECIDED ORCH w7 r5). The active rules are read from the review the pointer's record names (`active_policy`). Refusal: `POLICY_CONTRADICTS_INVARIANT` names the invariant IRI, the rule and the source clause. `POLICY_REVIEW_STALE` covers a changed source or invariant generation. `POLICY_CLAUSE_UNSUPPORTED` covers an inactive clause. `PROMOTION_CAS_CONFLICT` covers a raced revision. The guard (`guard_activation`, `check_activation`) runs after `qualified_champion` and before the pointer is written: the review must be the one the committed `promote` record cites and be for this scope and target (`POLICY_REVIEW_MISSING`, `POLICY_REVIEW_MISMATCH`), the invariant generation must be the reviewed one (`POLICY_REVIEW_STALE`), the active revision must be the `expected_policy_revision` of the review unless the set is already active (`PROMOTION_CAS_CONFLICT`), and no rule may contradict a published invariant, naming the first one in input order (`POLICY_CONTRADICTS_INVARIANT`). A `Decide` `Promotion` that names the review as `receipt_digest` reads it as evidence and, when the request names a `graph`, judges it against the candidates the caller sees now, so a source indexed again since the review abstains `POLICY_REVIEW_STALE`. All `GovernedSet` knobs are `Fixed`. Depends: R012.7.2, R012.8, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R805.11, ARBORA-OPS-R885. Caller chain: arbora `policy.review` (ARBORA-OPS-R885) returns a `ReviewApproval` → served `Decide` `Promotion` with subject `GovernedSet { scope_ref: policy:<repository> }` → `LearningWrite::MovePointer` (`crates/medulla-h-decide/src/decide/stat_learning.rs:181`) → `check_rule_against_invariants` (R129.3) before the move. Shape: new `crates/medulla-h-decide/src/decide/policy_activation.rs` with the activation guard, called by the promotion path of `stat_learning.rs`; it reuses `RuleInvariant` and `is_contradicted_by` (`crates/medulla-agent-types/src/agent_guardrail.rs:91`) and the `GovernedSet` subject of R805.10, so no second activation gate exists. | Test: `crates/medulla-h-decide/src/decide/stat_tests/policy_activation_tests.rs::activation_refuses_contradiction_and_stale_review` (L3). Test: `tests/orchestration/test_policy_activation_served.py::test_review_activate_and_decide_uses_active_rule` (L4). |

A section has a contiguous ordinal, `source_refs` and a content ref with digest. A source ref is `{entity_id, source_revision, source_digest, path, start_line, end_line}`. The six `WALKTHROUGH_*` codes are registered as `ServerErrorCode` variants beside the seams. | `crates/medulla-server-api/tests/walkthrough_reader_seams.rs::validation_reads_snapshot_and_blobs_only_through_the_seams` (L1). |

| `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R013.7.5` | **Production walkthrough readers installed at boot.** Stage: BUILD. Build order: this row lands before R013.7.3, which depends on it. The seams of R013.7.4 have no production implementation, so every served walkthrough write refuses `WALKTHROUGH_REVISION_STALE`. New file `crates/medulla-server/src/walkthrough_readers.rs` (`medulla-server` is the one crate that sees every handler crate, as `seams_install.rs` documents). `pub struct ServerBlobReader { state: Arc<RwLock<ServerState>> }` implements `BlobReader::read(tenant, artifact_ref)` by calling `medulla_server_blob::blob::engine_bodies::read_owned_blob(store, tenant, artifact_ref, WALKTHROUGH_ARTIFACT_MAX_BYTES)` over the engine blob chunk store of `ServerState.blob` and mapping any `Err` (`BODY_MISSING`, `READER_TOO_LARGE`) to `None`; `pub const WALKTHROUGH_ARTIFACT_MAX_BYTES: usize = 16 * 1024 * 1024`. `pub struct GraphIndexReader` implements `RepositoryIndexReader` over the committed repository index of the tenant graph: `current_revision(tenant, repository_id)` is the `base_revision` of the current complete generation (`RepositoryGeneration`, `crates/medulla-h-ingest-repository/src/repository_index/delta.rs`) of that repository, `None` when there is none; `snapshot_contains(tenant, repository_id, repository_revision, entity_id, source_revision, source_digest)` is true when `repository_revision` equals `current_revision` and the entity node `entity_id` exists in that generation with node properties `source_revision` and `source_digest` (lowercase hex of the SHA-256) equal to the arguments. The lowering in `crates/medulla-h-ingest-repository/src/repository_index.rs` writes those two properties on every entity node it emits (`source_revision` = the index request's target revision, `source_digest` = SHA-256 of the entity's source text). Another tenant's repository reads as `None`. `seams_install::install_seams` changes its parameter from `&ServerState` to `&Arc<RwLock<ServerState>>` (the tokio `RwLock` the server already owns and `install_seams_on` already takes); the engine boot call site passes that handle and no other caller exists. It calls `medulla_server_api::walkthrough_readers::install_walkthrough_readers(&state.seams, WalkthroughReaders::new(Arc::new(GraphIndexReader{..}), Arc::new(ServerBlobReader{..})))` in the `L6` slot, once. Each reader holds a clone of that `Arc`. The reader traits stay synchronous: a reader takes the lock with `try_read()` (never `blocking_read`, which panics inside the async `AddNode` path) for the duration of one read, and when the lock is not available it answers fail-closed (`BlobReader::read` returns `None`, `snapshot_contains` returns false, `current_revision` returns `None`), so the walkthrough write refuses `WALKTHROUGH_REVISION_STALE` and the caller retries. Caller chain: engine boot → `install_seams` → seam; served `AddNode` → `try_handle_gateway` → walkthrough read checks → these readers. Depends: R013.7.4, MEDULLA-REPO-INGEST-R006.4. Shape: one new file, one edit of `seams_install.rs` (the signature and its one call site), one edit of the lowering. | Test: `crates/medulla-server/tests/walkthrough_readers_installed.rs::installed_readers_confirm_a_current_revision_and_refuse_another_tenant` (L3): indexes a fixture repository for tenant A, then checks `current_revision`, `snapshot_contains` true for an indexed entity and false for a wrong digest, `None` for tenant B, and that `ServerBlobReader` returns the bytes of a blob owned by tenant A and `None` for tenant B. |
Depends: MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R013.7.4
| `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R013.7.2` | **Refusal: validate the manifest on write.** Refusals, each storing nothing: `WALKTHROUGH_REVISION_STALE` for a missing or stale repository revision. `WALKTHROUGH_REF_OUTSIDE_SNAPSHOT` for refs outside the pinned snapshot. `WALKTHROUGH_SECTION_UNCITED` for a section with no source ref. `WALKTHROUGH_ARTIFACT_UNRESOLVED` for an unresolved or tampered artifact. `WALKTHROUGH_NOTE_ORDER_INVALID` for a duplicate or gapped note index. `WALKTHROUGH_SPAN_OUTSIDE_RUN` for a span outside the run: a `WalkthroughNote` whose `run_id` differs from the walkthrough's `run_id`, or whose `step_index` is at or beyond the number of `steps` carried by the same `RunTrace` write. Seam installation: the handler resolves the three read checks through `WalkthroughReaders` installed on `state.seams.walkthrough` (R013.7.4, populated by R013.7.5). When no readers are installed the handler refuses `WALKTHROUGH_REVISION_STALE`, because no revision can be confirmed current (refusing is the safe answer; there is no pass-through). A cross-tenant ref reads as absent. Depends: R013.7.1. Caller chain: served `AddNode` → `try_handle_gateway` (`crates/medulla-h-graph-gateway/src/graph_ops/gateway.rs:57`) → `harness_admission::direct_add_node` (`crates/medulla-h-graph-gateway/src/graph_ops/gateway.rs:127`) → `harness_add_node_admission` (`crates/medulla-h-graph-gateway/src/harness_admission.rs:673`) → `validate_run_trace_node` (R009.15) → new `validate_walkthrough`. Admission stays graph-only. The repository-index and blob checks (`WALKTHROUGH_REVISION_STALE`, `WALKTHROUGH_REF_OUTSIDE_SNAPSHOT`, `WALKTHROUGH_ARTIFACT_UNRESOLVED`) run in the `AddNode` handler before admission, through the server-api read seams (the repository-index reader and the blob reader of `crates/medulla-server-api`); the handler passes their result to `validate_walkthrough` as a resolved-checks argument, and any failed check refuses before `harness_add_node_admission` runs. Shape: `validate_walkthrough` in `crates/medulla-h-graph-gateway/src/harness_admission.rs` beside `validate_step_records` (R013.3), reading only the graph and the resolved-checks argument; the six refusal codes are registered in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/error_registry.rs`; the planned unit test sits in `crates/medulla-h-graph-gateway/src/harness_admission/tests.rs`. | Test: `crates/medulla-h-graph-gateway/src/graph_ops/gateway_graph.rs::tests::add_run_trace_rejects_stale_walkthrough_source_or_artifact` (L3). |
Depends: MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R013.7.1, MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R013.7.4
| `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R013.7.3` | **Evidence: independent readback and artifact digest.** UQL readback returns the same ordered refs and digests as the write. The artifact read verifies its digest. Build order: R013.7.5 (the production readers) must land before this row, because without installed readers every walkthrough write refuses `WALKTHROUGH_REVISION_STALE` (R013.7.2) and this row's served test has nothing to read back. Scopes: the repository index lives in the tenant graph `tenant__<tenant>` (a client indexes with `index_repository(..., graph="tenant__<tenant>")`; the index reader scans `tenant__<tenant>` and `tenant__<tenant>__*`), and the artifact blob is owned by the carrier owner scope, because that is how every served upload is stored (`opaque_coordinator_key("carrier-owner", ..)`, `crates/medulla-h-ingest/src/kv/dispatch_handlers.rs`). The blob owner is never the tenant. Seams: `BlobReader::read(&self, owner_scope: &str, artifact_ref: &str) -> Option<Vec<u8>>` and `WalkthroughReaders::resolve(&self, tenant: &str, blob_owner: &str, walkthrough: &CodeWalkthrough)` (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/walkthrough_readers.rs`) take the owner scope; `resolve_walkthrough_checks(method, tenant, blob_owner, readers)` (`crates/medulla-h-graph-gateway/src/harness_admission.rs:898`) passes it, and its two callers in `graph_ops/gateway.rs:148` and `graph_ops/gateway_graph.rs:1659` pass the writing request's carrier owner scope, the empty string when the request has no verified carrier, so the artifact then reads as absent and the write refuses `WALKTHROUGH_ARTIFACT_UNRESOLVED`. The index reader seam is unchanged. Caller chain: arbora `ask` → `Method::Uql` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:138`) readback; the artifact read goes through the blob read of `crates/medulla-h-ingest-repository/src/blob.rs`. Proof level: the served Python session test (the carrier-owned upload and the tenant-graph index are both reachable from a served client); no production code or route is added. Shape: one test fn in `tests/orchestration/test_code_walkthrough_served.py`: it indexes a fixture file with `index_repository(..., graph="tenant__<tenant>")`, takes an entity node whose `source_digest` property the index stamped (hex, with or without the `sha256:` prefix; the test strips the prefix), uploads the artifact with `client.blob.store` (owned by the carrier owner scope), writes a `RunTrace` node whose `walkthrough` property cites that entity, the artifact ref `cas:sha256:<hex>` and the artifact digest, reads the node back with `Method::Uql` and asserts the ordered refs and digests equal the write, then fetches the artifact with `client.blob.fetch` and asserts its SHA-256 equals the stored `artifact_digest`. | Test: `tests/orchestration/test_code_walkthrough_served.py::test_walkthrough_trace_and_artifact_read_back` (L4). |
Depends: MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R013.7.2, MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R013.7.5

Types in `crates/medulla-work-types/src/run_completion.rs`: `pub enum TimingPhase { Request, Run, Queue, Tool }` (`Copy`, `#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]`, a label set at construction, so no machine); `pub struct PhaseTiming { pub phase: TimingPhase, pub duration_ms: u64 }` (`deny_unknown_fields`); `pub const MAX_PHASE_DURATION_MS: u64 = 86_400_000;`. `RunCompletionInput` gains `pub timings: Vec<PhaseTiming>` with `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]`, so a completion without timings keeps its bytes and its `run_completion_digest`, the lowercase hex SHA-256 of `rmp_serde::to_vec_named(&RunCompletionPayload)` (`crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/commit.rs:303`). Enforcement at the chokepoint `RunCompletionInput::validate`, which `domain_scope.rs:28`, `commit.rs:802` and `run_owner.rs:179` already call: more than one entry for a phase, or a `duration_ms` above `MAX_PHASE_DURATION_MS`, refuses `INVALID_ARGUMENT: invalid run completion timing: <phase> <detail>` and the commit writes nothing. The generated Python models are regenerated from the contract schema. Writes: the timings inside the one committed completion. Consumer: the session usage read of MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R805. Caller chain: the run harness → `Method::CommitWorkItemResult { run_completion: Some(RunCompletionInput { timings, .. }), .. }` → `domain_scope` admission (`RunCompletionInput::validate`) → `commit_work_item_result` (`commit.rs`) → `validate_ordinary_completion` → the `RunCompletionPayload` write and its digest. Shape: one enum, one struct, one constant and one field in `run_completion.rs`; no new method, store or log op. | Test: `crates/medulla-work-types/src/run_completion.rs::tests::timings_validate_and_keep_the_empty_digest` (L1) asserts that a completion without timings encodes to the same bytes as before, that a duplicate phase and an over-day duration refuse, and that four distinct phases pass. |

It orders the runs by `(commit_sequence, run_id)`, gives turn indexes `0..` in that order, and emits one record per stored phase in `TimingPhase` declaration order. | `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R806` | **Policy activation op with review receipt.** Stage: write path, the `DecisionLog` op `policy_activation { tenant_id, change, source, receipt_digest }` with `change` in `create`, `update`, `retire` (`DecisionLogOp::PolicyActivation`, class `Govern`; DECIDED ORCH w7 r5: an op of the one native decision write route, not a second `Method`, so it adds no second capability row, mutation-plan entry or scope). `source` is the repository and `receipt_digest` the review receipt recorded by `learn` `record_policy_review` (R012.8). A `create` names no replaced revision and an `update` names the one it replaces (`expected_policy_revision`), else `POLICY_REVIEW_MISMATCH`. `create` and `update` move the pointer `champion:governed_set:policy:<repository>` to `policyset:<candidate_set_digest>` by the one compare-and-set of `stat_pointer::activate`; `retire` requires the review to name the active set and returns the pointer to the revision before it (none when it was the first), and is held back by no invariant. | `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R808` | **The agent definition node carries a knowledge scope.** Stage: vocabulary. Parent row: `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R800.1`. The agent definition is the graph node of type `AgentDefinition` that the retrieval read side resolves by `agent_ref` (an Agent Library entry is a different record and holds no scope). The node's property `knowledgeScope` holds a `KnowledgeScope` as a JSON object or its JSON text. `KnowledgeScope`, `ScopedCandidate`, `parse_knowledge_scope` and `unknown_set` live in new `crates/medulla-work-types/src/orchestration/knowledge_scope.rs`; `crates/medulla-retrieval/src/knowledge_scope.rs` re-exports them and reads the scope through the same parser. A scope naming a knowledge set that no node of the writer's graph holds, or that another tenant's node holds, refuses `SCOPE_SET_UNKNOWN: knowledge set <id> is unknown`, byte-identical for an absent and a foreign set (no existence oracle); a malformed scope refuses `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. Both are datatype properties because the importer writes them as node property text, like `dmnTypeRef`. In `crates/medulla-server-api/src/schema_compose.rs` the dmn module pin moves by exactly +9 triples. Shapes in `crates/medulla-core/ontology/dmn-v1.shapes.ttl` (existing file): `:DmnDecisionTableShape` gains `sh:minCount 1` on its `:dmnHitPolicy` property (exactly one with the existing `sh:maxCount 1`) and one `sh:sparql` constraint, aggregate-free like `business_ops-v1.shapes.ttl`, with `sh:message "A dmnAggregation appears only with the COLLECT hit policy."` and `sh:select "SELECT $this WHERE { $this :dmnAggregation ?a ; :dmnHitPolicy ?p . FILTER(?p != \"COLLECT\") }"`; a new `:DmnDecisionShape` with `sh:targetClass :DmnDecision` and one `sh:sparql` constraint with `sh:message "A dmnRequiresDecision chain does not return to its start."` and `sh:select "SELECT $this WHERE { $this :dmnRequiresDecision+ $this }"`; a new `:DmnRuleShape` with `sh:targetClass :DmnRule`, `sh:property [ sh:path :dmnTable ; sh:minCount 1 ; sh:maxCount 1 ; sh:datatype xsd:string ]` and `sh:property [ sh:path :dmnRuleIndex ; sh:minCount 1 ; sh:maxCount 1 ]`. The file gains `@prefix xsd: <http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#> .`, and the prefix declaration `:DmnShapesPrefixes sh:declare [ sh:prefix "" ; sh:namespace "https://knuckles-team.github.io/ontology/core#"^^xsd:anyURI ]` is added and named by each `sh:prefixes`. Caller chain: server bootstrap → `crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/schema_sources.rs` composes the `dmn` `CoreSpec` → the shapes run on each `MutationBatch` of MEDULLA-CQ-ORCH-R702.9; the import path is `ProcessModelImport` (MEDULLA-CQ-ORCH-R701.3) → `parse_dmn` → `table`. UQL has no join from a record's state text to a machine description, and the machine description is loaded as schema triples in the `__commons__` graph (R811.5), not as nodes a `MATCH` can start from. So the question is two served reads, with no join between data and schema. (1) The caller reads the record's state text with `GetNode` (for a work item, the `status` property; the machine IRI is the one the record's kind cites, for a work item `:WorkItemMachine`). (2) One `Method::Sparql` (`crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_07.rs`) against graph `__commons__`, `base_iri` empty, `requires_packs` empty, with the statement text from `next_events_sparql(machine_iri: &str, state_text: &str) -> Result<String, String>` in the new file `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/state_machine_next.rs`. The function checks `machine_iri` has no character of `<>"{}|\^` or whitespace and `state_text` is non-empty with no `"` or `\`, else returns `Err("INVALID_ARGUMENT: ...")`; it then fills this fixed text: `SELECT ?edge ?event ?guard ?to WHERE { <machine> :hasState ?from . ?from :storedForm "<state_text>" . ?t :fromState ?from ; :firedBy ?event ; :toState ?target ; :edgeId ?edge . ?target :storedForm ?to . OPTIONAL { ?t :guardedBy ?guard } } ORDER BY ?edge`, with the `:` prefix declared as the core namespace. A row is one edge: its id, event, guard (unbound when none) and target stored text. A state with no outgoing edge returns no row. The function is the only place the text exists. No Python test is written: the Python client has no work item create route and none is added for a test, and a second copy of the text would be a second route; the proof is a Rust served test that calls `next_events_sparql` itself. Caller chain: arbora `ask` → engine client `sparql` → `Method::Sparql` → the existing SPARQL handler; no new `Method`, code or registration. Writes: none. Depends: R811.5, R812.3. The statechart mirror only alarms on divergence. This rollup makes `WorkItemStatus::apply` the only writer. It stores the enum form only. It drops the legacy `completed` literal in the same release. The state vocabulary is exported for consumers by R812.5. Lease shape: Leased and Running mean the lease is Active. Commit, Defer and Cancel release it. LeaseExpire reclaims it. | `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R812.1` | **Work item transition function and edge table.** Stage: model. Add `crates/medulla-work-types/src/work_item_machine.rs` beside `work_item_read.rs`. `WorkItemStatus::apply(event, ctx) -> Result<WorkItemStatus, TransitionRefused>` is the one transition function. `WorkItemStatus::is_terminal` is the only terminal test. A terminal state accepts no event. The initial state comes from `WorkItemStatus::initial(open_dependencies)`. Machine `WorkItemMachine`. States: Submitted, Ready, Leased, Running, Succeeded, Failed, Cancelled, DeadLetter. Initial state: Submitted (the one `:initialState` of R811.3). With no open dependency, W01 fires at admission, so a submit with no open dependency stores Ready. Terminal states: Succeeded, Failed, Cancelled (reached by Cancel or Commit(cancelled), and the state a requested stop maps to), DeadLetter. Edges follow; every other pair of state and event is refused. W01: Submitted to Ready on DepsSatisfied, guard: no dependency is open. W02: Ready to Leased on Claim, guard: the next retry time has passed and attempts are below the maximum. W03: Leased to Running on Start, guard: the owner, epoch and fence match and the lease is live. W04: Running to Running on Heartbeat, guard: the owner, epoch and fence match and the lease is live. W05: Running to Succeeded on Commit(succeeded), guard: the owner, epoch and fence match and the lease is live. W06: Running to Failed on Commit(failed, final), guard: the owner, epoch and fence match and the lease is live. W07: Running to Cancelled on Commit(cancelled), guard: the owner, epoch and fence match and the lease is live. W08: Running to Ready on Commit(failed, retryable), guard: the owner, epoch and fence match and the lease is live and attempts are below the maximum. W09: Running to DeadLetter on Commit(failed, retryable), guard: the owner, epoch and fence match and the lease is live and attempts reached the maximum. W10: Leased to Succeeded on Commit(succeeded), guard: the owner, epoch and fence match and the lease is live. W11: Leased to Failed on Commit(failed, final), guard: the owner, epoch and fence match and the lease is live. W12: Leased to Cancelled on Commit(cancelled), guard: the owner, epoch and fence match and the lease is live. W13: Leased to Ready on Commit(failed, retryable), guard: the owner, epoch and fence match and the lease is live and attempts are below the maximum. W14: Leased to DeadLetter on Commit(failed, retryable), guard: the owner, epoch and fence match and the lease is live and attempts reached the maximum. W15: Submitted to Cancelled on Cancel, no guard. W16: Ready to Cancelled on Cancel, no guard. W17: Leased to Cancelled on Cancel, guard: the lease has expired. W18: Running to Cancelled on Cancel, guard: the lease has expired. W19: Leased to Leased on RequestCancel, guard: the lease is live; the event stamps `cancel_requested_at_ms` only. W20: Running to Running on RequestCancel, guard: the lease is live; the event stamps `cancel_requested_at_ms` only. W21: Leased to Ready on Defer, guard: the owner, epoch and fence match and the lease is live and neither counter overflows. W22: Running to Ready on Defer, guard: the owner, epoch and fence match and the lease is live and neither counter overflows. W23: Leased to Ready on LeaseExpire(retry), guard: the lease has expired and attempts are below the maximum. W24: Running to Ready on LeaseExpire(retry), guard: the lease has expired and attempts are below the maximum. W25: Leased to DeadLetter on LeaseExpire(exhausted), guard: the lease has expired and attempts reached the maximum. W26: Running to DeadLetter on LeaseExpire(exhausted), guard: the lease has expired and attempts reached the maximum. Refusal: `TransitionRefused` with fields `machine` (`WorkItemMachine`), `from` (stored state text), `event` and `reason`; code `TRANSITION_REFUSED`, defined in `crates/medulla-types/src/transition_refused.rs` (MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R811.4). Persisted state: `status` (stored text of `WorkItemStatus`, `crates/medulla-work-types/src/work_item_read.rs:88`); `is_terminal` and `lease_is_live(now)` are derived. Transition function: `WorkItemStatus::apply` in `crates/medulla-work-types/src/work_item_machine.rs`. Lease shape: Leased and Running mean the lease is Active. Commit, Defer and Cancel release it. LeaseExpire reclaims it. Expiry is derived: `lease_is_live(now)` compares `lease_expires_at` with the clock, and no work item state is named Expired. Caller chain: the redb store writers of R812.2 → `apply`. Writes: none. Depends: MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R811.4. Shape: one new file; the edge table is one `const` that the ontology test of R812.3 reads. | Test: `crates/medulla-work-types/src/work_item_machine.rs::tests::edge_w01_submitted_deps_satisfied`, `crates/medulla-work-types/src/work_item_machine.rs::tests::edge_w02_ready_claim`, `crates/medulla-work-types/src/work_item_machine.rs::tests::edge_w03_leased_start`, The wire contract is the typed outcome, not an error: `commit_work_item_result_precheck` (`work_item/commit.rs`) answers `WorkItemCommitOutcome::Noop` for a commit on a terminal (Cancelled) item and `WorkItemCommitOutcome::Fenced` for a commit whose `fencing_token` or lease is stale, both before `fire` runs; `apply_cancel_work_item_row` (`work_item/cancel.rs`, `cancel_status_refusal`) answers `WorkItemCancelOutcome::Noop` for a `CancelWorkItem` on a terminal (Succeeded) item. These outcomes are the current state and do not change (no wire change; clients that read `Noop` and `Fenced` keep working). Only an event the machine does not allow, reaching `fire` directly, answers `TRANSITION_REFUSED`; that path is proven by R812.3. | `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R812.5` | **Export the work item state vocabulary.** Stage: exported contract. `crates/medulla-work-types/src/work_item_machine.rs` exports `WorkItemStatus` with eight stored texts (`submitted`, `ready`, `leased`, `running`, `succeeded`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `dead_letter`), `WorkItemEvent`, `WorkItemStatus::is_terminal` and `WorkItemTerminalReason`. `WorkItemStatus::terminal_reason` returns a reason for a terminal state and none otherwise. The mapping is Succeeded to `succeeded`, Failed to `failed`, Cancelled to `cancelled` and DeadLetter to `retries_exhausted`. Cancelled is the one terminal state of a requested stop: a consumer maps a user-requested stop (the meristem `Stopped(requested)`) to Cancelled with reason `cancelled`, and a budget stop to Failed with reason `failed`. No consumer adds a second terminal state or reason for the same outcome. The ontology exports `:WorkItemMachine` and the state instances `:WorkItemMachine_<State>`. Consumers map their own states onto these names and keep no copy of the list. The run machine that gives `Stopped(reason)` its reason (MERISTEM-HARNESS-CYCLE-R084) and the run reads of ARBORA-OPS-R900 and ARBORA-OPS-R902 cite this row. Caller chain: consumers import the crate or read the ontology through Uql. Writes: none. Depends: R812.1, R812.3. | `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R815.9` | **WorkflowRegistrationEntry/WorkflowLifecycle is one explicit state machine, and the register upsert routes through its transition function.** Stage: model, store cutover and ontology (organization constitution Article XXXI). Language: Rust. Machine `WorkflowMachine` in new `crates/medulla-work-types/src/orchestration/workflow_machine.rs`. States: `WorkflowLifecycle` (`crates/medulla-work-types/src/orchestration/workflows.rs:72`) with stored texts `active`, `stale`, `withdrawn`. Initial: `Active`. Terminal: `Withdrawn`. Events: `WorkflowEvent { MarkStale, Withdraw, Reactivate }`, names `mark_stale`, `withdraw`, `reactivate`. Edges: F01 `Active` `MarkStale` → `Stale`; F02 `Active` `Withdraw` → `Withdrawn`; F03 `Stale` `Withdraw` → `Withdrawn`. `Reactivate` has no edge: the identity carries its content digest, so a stale or withdrawn version never becomes active again. A new version is a new identity. Writer: `admit_registration` (`crates/medulla-h-graph-gateway/src/graph_ops/workflow_registry.rs:43`). With no stored node, the requested lifecycle must be `WORKFLOW_MACHINE.initial`, or the register refuses `INVALID_ARGUMENT: a first registration is active`. With a stored node and the same lifecycle, it stays the idempotent `Admitted::Same`. With a different lifecycle, it fires the event of the requested state (`stale` → `MarkStale`, `withdrawn` → `Withdraw`, `active` → `Reactivate`) on the stored state. A refusal writes nothing. Add `WorkflowRegistry` to `MACHINE_METHODS`. Decoding a stored row uses `WORKFLOW_MACHINE.state_from_stored`; `WorkflowLifecycle::parse` is deleted. Persisted state: one field, `WorkflowRegistrationEntry.lifecycle` (`workflows.rs:102`). Shape: one `static WORKFLOWMACHINE: MachineDef<..>` edge table and `StateMachineApply` impl, mirroring `crates/medulla-catalog-source-types/src/probe_machine.rs`. `apply` calls `medulla_types::transition_refused::transition`; it is the only writer of the state. Ontology: add `:WorkflowMachine` of `:StateMachine` to `crates/medulla-core/ontology/state-machines-v1.ttl` with one state per variant (`:storedForm`), one transition per edge id, and the five subclasses of R811.6. The state-machine pins of `complete_core_corpus_pins_the_approved_authority_migration_delta` (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/schema_compose.rs`) move by the measured delta. Caller chain: served `WorkflowRegistry` `Register` → `handle_workflow_register` → `admit_registration` → `WorkflowLifecycle::apply` → the `AddNode` upsert. Writes: the state field only. Depends: MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R811.4, MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R811.8, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R166.3. | Test: `crates/medulla-work-types/src/orchestration/workflow_machine.rs::tests::every_legal_transition_applies` and `::tests::every_illegal_pair_is_refused_with_transition_refused` (L1). Test: `crates/medulla-h-graph-gateway/src/graph_ops/workflow_registry_tests.rs::reactivating_a_withdrawn_workflow_refuses` (L1). Test: `crates/medulla-rdf/tests/state_machine_vocabulary.rs::workflow_machine_matches_edge_table` (planned) (L1). |

| `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R811` | **Run metrics read (rollup).** Arbora reports cost, tokens and solved counts of a tenant's runs from the measurements the engine already holds. Children deliver it. | Root tests are the R811 children. |
| `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R811.1` | **`RunMetrics`.** Caller chain: served `Method::RunMetrics {request: {tenant, query, group_by?, window?}}` → `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs` → `handle_run_metrics` (`crates/medulla-h-query-read/src/query/run_metrics.rs`) → `medulla_types::run_metrics::aggregate`. Rule: a read under `telemetry:read` over the `:RunTrace` nodes of the caller's projected graph (`costUsd`, `uncachedInputTokens`, `cachedInputTokens`, `outputTokens`, `terminationReason`, `completedAtMs`, `agentId`, `projectId`). `query` is `cost` or `tokens` (each per solved run, a solved run being `terminationReason` `final_answer`) or `solved`; `group_by` is `agent`, `project` or `termination`, else one group `all`; `window` keeps runs completed in `[start_ms, end_ms)`. The answer is `{query, groups: [{key, value >= 0, trace_count}], abstained?}`. A population with no solved run answers `abstained` `NO_SOLVED_RUNS` with no groups, and one whose solved runs report no such measurement answers `NO_MEASUREMENTS`; neither answers a zero. A tenant other than the verified one refuses `ACCESS_DENIED`, an empty window `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. Python mirror: `MethodRunMetrics`, `coordination.send_run_metrics`, `GraphOperationsClient.run_metrics`. | Tests: `crates/medulla-types/src/run_metrics.rs::tests::a_population_without_a_solved_run_abstains_and_never_reports_a_zero_cost` (L1) and `crates/medulla-h-query-read/src/query/run_metrics.rs::tests::a_run_trace_node_reads_into_its_sample_and_other_nodes_are_not_runs` (L2).; `tests/test_run_metrics_served.py::test_run_metrics_groups_solved_runs_and_refuses_a_foreign_tenant` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R811.1.1` | **A technique measure is `RunMetrics` with a technique, arm and configuration filter.** Class: BUILD. There is no separate technique-measure method: a measure of one benchmark arm is the existing `RunMetrics` read narrowed to the run traces whose `techniqueId`, `armId` and `configDigest` match the optional request fields `technique`, `arm` and `config_digest` (each 1 to 256 bytes, else `INVALID_ARGUMENT`), so it is one more route to nothing. The query set gains `latency` (mean `latencyMs` of the solved runs that report one) and `score` (mean `benchmarkScore` of the same), beside `cost`, `tokens` and `solved`; `group_by` gains `technique`, `arm` and `config`. The three queries over the same filter are the latency, cost and score of one `(technique, arm, config_digest)`. A population with no solved run, or with none reporting the asked counter, abstains and never answers zero. Code: `crates/medulla-types/src/run_metrics.rs` (`RunMetricsRequest::admits`, `aggregate`), `crates/medulla-h-query-read/src/query/run_metrics.rs` (`sample_of`). | Test: `crates/medulla-types/src/run_metrics.rs::a_technique_measure_is_latency_cost_and_score_of_one_technique_arm_and_config` (L1); served proof `tests/test_run_metrics_served.py::test_run_metrics_filters_one_technique_arm_and_config` (L3). |
